<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove the Eisenbud–Green–Harris conjecture over every characteristic-zero field for homogeneous regular sequences of any positive length, with degrees at least two. Thus every homogeneous ideal containing such a sequence has the Hilbert function of a monomial ideal containing the corresponding pure powers.

- **claim-commuting-form-construction** (construction, [source](#stmt-thm-construction)): Let $n\ge1$, let $2\le a_1\le\cdots\le a_n$, and let $f_1,\ldots,f_n$ be a homogeneous regular sequence in $\mathbb C[x_1,\ldots,x_n]$ with $\deg f_i=a_i$. There exist a finitely generated field extension $k/\mathbb C$, a finite-dimensional central division $k$-algebra $\Delta$, and a commutative graded $k$-subalgebra $$k[x_1,\ldots,x_n]\subseteq A\subseteq\Delta[x_1,\ldots,x_n]$$ generated by finitely many pairwise commuting linear forms, with distinguished $t_1,\ldots,t_n\in A_1$, such that $$\label{eq:construction-triangular} t_i^{a_i}=c_i f_i+\sum_{l<i}B_{il}f_l+\sum_{d>i}C_{id}t_d \qquad(1\le i\le n).$$ Here $c_i\in k^\times$, $B_{il}\in A_{a_i-a_l}$, and $C_{id}\in A_{a_i-1}$. Moreover, $A$ is finite over $k[x]$, and $$\{t_1^{\alpha_1}\cdots t_n^{\alpha_n}: \alpha\in\mathbb Z_{\ge0}^n\}$$ is a basis of the full polynomial algebra $\Delta[x]$ as a left $\Delta$-vector space.
  - Explanation: For a complex homogeneous regular sequence of degrees at least two, there are a division algebra over a finitely generated extension field and pairwise commuting linear forms satisfying triangular power identities. Their ordered monomials form a left basis of the whole polynomial algebra.
- **claim-artinian-egh** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): Let $n\ge1$ and $2\le a_1\le\cdots\le a_n$. Suppose that a homogeneous ideal $I\subseteq\mathbb C[x_1,\ldots,x_n]$ contains a regular sequence $f_1,\ldots,f_n$ with $\deg f_i=a_i$. There is a monomial ideal $J\subseteq\mathbb C[x_1,\ldots,x_n]$ such that $$x_i^{a_i}\in J\quad(1\le i\le n), \qquad H_{S/J}(d)=H_{S/I}(d)\quad\text{for every }d\ge0.$$
  - Explanation: Any homogeneous ideal over the complex numbers containing a regular sequence of the given degrees has the Hilbert function of a monomial ideal containing the pure powers.
- **claim-egh-characteristic-zero** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-characteristic-zero)): Let $F$ be a field of characteristic zero, let $S_F=F[x_1,\ldots,x_n]$ be standard graded, and let $$1\le c\le n,\qquad 2\le a_1\le\cdots\le a_c.$$ If a homogeneous ideal $I\subseteq S_F$ contains a homogeneous regular sequence $f_1,\ldots,f_c$ with $\deg f_i=a_i$, there is a monomial ideal $J\subseteq S_F$ containing $P_F=(x_1^{a_1},\ldots,x_c^{a_c})$ such that $$\dim_F(S_F/J)_d=\dim_F(S_F/I)_d\qquad(d\ge0).$$ Moreover, $J$ may be chosen lex-plus-powers: in each degree its monomials outside $P_F$ are the lexicographically greatest $\dim_F I_d-\dim_F(P_F)_d$ monomials outside $P_F$, for the order $x_1>\cdots>x_n$.
  - Explanation: Extends the Hilbert function comparison, together with a lex-plus-powers choice of the monomial ideal, to regular sequences of any length over fields of characteristic zero.
- **claim-ordered-basis-criterion** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-ordered-basis)): Let $f_1,\ldots,f_n$ be a homogeneous regular sequence in $S=\mathbb C[x_1,\ldots,x_n]$, with $\deg f_i=a_i>0$. Suppose that a field extension $k/\mathbb C$ and a finite-dimensional central division $k$-algebra $\Delta$ have the following property. There is a finite collection of pairwise commuting elements of $\Delta[x_1,\ldots,x_n]_1$, containing $x_1,\ldots,x_n$, whose generated commutative $k$-algebra $A$ contains elements $t_1,\ldots,t_n$ of degree one satisfying $$\label{eq:triangular} t_i^{a_i} =c_i f_i+\sum_{l<i}B_{il}f_l+\sum_{d>i}C_{id}t_d, \qquad 1\le i\le n,$$ where $c_i\in k^\times$ and all terms are homogeneous, with $B_{il},C_{id}\in A$. Then $A$ is finite over $k[x_1,\ldots,x_n]$, and $$\{t_1^{\alpha_1}\cdots t_n^{\alpha_n}:\alpha\in\mathbb Z_{\ge0}^n\}$$ is a basis of $\Delta[x_1,\ldots,x_n]$ as a left $\Delta$-vector space.
  - Explanation: Triangular power identities among commuting linear forms force the forms to be a regular system of parameters, which yields an ordered left basis of the polynomial algebra over the division algebra.
- **claim-monomial-extraction** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-monomial-reduction)): Let $f_1,\ldots,f_n$, $k$, $\Delta$, $A$, and $t_1,\ldots,t_n$ satisfy the hypotheses of Lemma <a href="#lem:ordered-basis" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:ordered-basis">4</a>. For every homogeneous ideal $I\subseteq S=\mathbb C[x_1,\ldots,x_n]$ containing the $f_i$, there is one monomial ideal $J\subseteq S$ such that $$x_i^{a_i}\in J\quad(1\le i\le n),\qquad \dim_\mathbb C(S/J)_d=\dim_\mathbb C(S/I)_d\quad(d\ge0).$$
  - Explanation: Least exponents of elements of the extended ideal form an upward-closed set, which defines one monomial ideal with the same Hilbert function and containing the pure powers.
- **claim-stage-basepoint-free** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-stage-basepoint)): The space $\mathfrak j_b$ is base-point-free on $\mathbb P(U^\vee)$.
  - Explanation: The degree-b part of the stage ideal has no base point, because a common zero would propagate through the triangular rows and force all variables to vanish.
- **claim-simple-factor** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-simple-factor)): Let $U$ be a vector space of dimension at least three over a characteristic-zero field, and let $b\ge2$. Suppose that $Q\subseteq\mathop{\mathrm{Sym}}U$ is generated in degrees one and two, and that its geometric characteristic set contains no integral curve of degree at most $b(b-1)$. Let $\mathfrak j_b\subseteq\mathop{\mathrm{Sym}}^b U$ be a base-point-free subspace containing $(Q)_b$, and define $W$ by <a href="#eq:stage-annihilator" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:stage-annihilator">[eq:stage-annihilator]</a>. Then every nonzero element of $W$, after any algebraically closed extension, has an irreducible factor of multiplicity one.
  - Explanation: If the characteristic set contains no integral curve of small degree, every nonzero annihilating polynomial has an irreducible factor of multiplicity one.
- **claim-bounded-curve-field** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-bounded-curve-field)): For integers $n\ge2$ and $D\ge1$, there is an integer $d_*(n,D)$ with the following property. If $\Omega$ is an algebraically closed extension of $\mathbb C$ and $Z\subseteq\mathbb P^{n-1}_\Omega$ is an integral curve of degree at most $D$, then $Z$ is defined over an algebraically closed intermediate field $F_0\subseteq\Omega$ with $\operatorname{trdeg}_\mathbb CF_0\le d_*(n,D)$.
  - Explanation: Every integral curve of bounded degree is defined over an algebraically closed field of uniformly bounded transcendence degree, via the incidence description of the Chow form.
- **claim-initial-data** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-initial-data)): For every $n\ge1$ and $D\ge1$, there exist data $k,\Delta_0,U,Q$ satisfying conditions <span class="upright">(i)</span>–<span class="upright">(iii)</span> above and the condition on $\mathcal C(Q)$. They may be chosen with $$U=\langle x_1,\ldots,x_n,s_1,\ldots,s_m\rangle_k, \qquad Q=(s_\rho^2-q_\rho(x):1\le\rho\le m),$$ where $m\ge2$ and the coefficient vectors of the quadrics $q_\rho$ are jointly algebraically independent over $\mathbb C$.
  - Explanation: Initial induction data exist, built from a skew Laurent algebra and independent generic quadrics, such that the characteristic set contains no curve of the bounded degree.
- **claim-sparsity-preserved** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-sparsity-preserved)): Let $U\subseteq\widetilde U$ be finite-dimensional vector spaces over a field, and let $Q\subseteq\mathop{\mathrm{Sym}}U$ and $\widetilde Q\subseteq\mathop{\mathrm{Sym}}\widetilde U$ be homogeneous ideals, with $Q\subseteq\widetilde Q$. Suppose that $\mathop{\mathrm{Sym}}\widetilde U/\widetilde Q$ is finite over $\mathop{\mathrm{Sym}}U/Q$. Then the induced projection of characteristic sets is everywhere defined and finite, and pulls back $\mathcal O(1)$ to $\mathcal O(1)$. In particular, if the first characteristic set contains no integral curve of degree at most $D$ after algebraically closed extension, neither does the second.
  - Explanation: A finite graded extension of the relation algebra induces a finite projection of characteristic sets pulling back the hyperplane bundle, so exclusion of small curves is inherited.
- **claim-homogeneous-joins** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-homogeneous-joins)): Let $w$ be a nonzero homogeneous polynomial of degree $b\ge2$ on a complex vector space $U$. Suppose that some irreducible factor of $w$ has multiplicity one. Then $w^{-1}(1)$ is connected. For every $M\ge1$, the smooth hypersurface $$F_w=\left\{(\ell_1,\ldots,\ell_M)\in U^{\oplus M}: \sum_{m=1}^M w(\ell_m)=1\right\}$$ has the homotopy type of the join of $M$ copies of $w^{-1}(1)$. Consequently, $$\widetilde H_j(F_w;\mathbb Z)=0\qquad(0\le j\le2M-2).$$
  - Explanation: The nonzero level of a homogeneous polynomial with a simple factor is connected, and the level hypersurface of a sum of many copies has the homotopy type of an iterated join, hence vanishing low reduced homology.
- **claim-parameter-spaces** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-parameters)): Let $k$ be a field admitting an embedding into $\mathbb C$, let $U$ be a finite-dimensional $k$-vector space with $\dim U\ge3$, and let $$W\subseteq\mathop{\mathrm{Sym}}^b(U^\vee),\qquad b\ge2,\qquad s=\dim W.$$ Assume that every nonzero element of $W_{\bar k}$ has an irreducible factor of multiplicity one over an algebraic closure $\bar k$. For integers $h\ge2$ and $M\ge2h+4s+10$, put $N_*=M\dim U$ and define $$\begin{split} Y&=\left\{(\ell_1,\ldots,\ell_M)\in U^{\oplus M}: \sum_{m=1}^M w(\ell_m)=0\quad(w\in W)\right\},\\ X&=\mathbb P(Y)\subseteq\mathbb P(U^{\oplus M}). \end{split}$$ Then $X$ is a geometrically integral complete intersection of codimension $s$, cut out by $s$ equations of degree $b$, and $$\operatorname{codim}_X\operatorname{Sing}X \ge M-2s+1>h,$$ with the codimension of the empty locus understood to be infinite. In particular, a general $h$-dimensional projective linear section is a smooth complete intersection contained in [(eq:parameter-jacobian)](#eq:parameter-jacobian)$X_{\mathrm{sm}}$.* *After any embedding $k\hookrightarrow\mathbb C$, let $Y^o$ be the nonzero cone over $X_{\mathrm{sm}}$. Both $Y^o$ and its unit-norm retract are $(M+2h)$-connected.
  - Explanation: The parameter variety is a geometrically integral complete intersection with high-codimension singular locus, and the cone over its smooth locus is highly connected.
- **claim-binary-addition-data** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-binary-curves)): Let $b\ge2$, fix a primitive $b$-th root $\zeta$, and put $$G=(\mu_b^b/\mu_b)\rtimes(\mathbb Z/b),\qquad e=b^b,$$ where the denominator is the diagonal subgroup and $\mathbb Z/b$ permutes the $b$ factors cyclically. There are smooth connected projective curves $C,T_0$, a connected étale $G$-cover $C\to T_0$, and a genuinely $G$-linearized line bundle $L$ on $C$ with the following properties.* 1. *The genera and degree satisfy $$g_T:=g(T_0)=b-1,\qquad g(C)-1=e(g_T-1),\qquad \deg L=e.$$* 2. *There are sections $a_\alpha\in H^0(C,L)$, indexed by $\alpha\in\mathbb Z_{\ge0}^b$ with $\|\alpha\|=b$, whose transport follows the degree-$b$ monomial representation in formal homogeneous coordinates $p_0,\ldots,p_{b-1}$. The notation $p^\alpha$ labels the section $a_\alpha$ when $\|\alpha\|=b$; it does not assign individual sections of $L$ to the $p_j$. Write $a_\alpha=p^\alpha$ and set $$z=\frac1b\sum_{j=0}^{b-1}p_j^b,\qquad u=-\frac1b\sum_{j=0}^{b-1}\zeta^{-j}p_j^b,\qquad v=p_0\cdots p_{b-1}.$$ They satisfy $$\label{eq:binary-section-relations} p_j^b=z-\zeta^ju,\qquad z^b=u^b+v^b.$$ The section $z$ is invariant, and $u,v$ have characters $\chi,\psi:G\to\mu_b$, respectively. The pencil $u,v$ is base-point-free and defines a degree-$e$ morphism $\pi:C\to\mathbb P^1$ with $L=\pi^*\mathcal O(1)$.* 3. *For a generic $A\in\mathop{\mathrm{Pic}}^0(C)$, over its field of definition $\kappa$, the space $E_A=H^0(C,A L^{g_T})$ has dimension $e$. Multiplication of sections gives isomorphisms $$\label{eq:curve-free-module} H^0(C,A L^{g_T+j}) =E_A\otimes_\kappa\mathop{\mathrm{Sym}}^j_\kappa\langle u,v\rangle \qquad(j\ge0).$$ Every $a\in H^0(C,L)$ consequently acts by a homogeneous linear matrix polynomial $M_a(U,V)\in\mathop{\mathrm{End}}_\kappa(E_A)[U,V]$. These matrix polynomials commute, preserve all polynomial identities among the sections, and satisfy $$M_u=U\,\mathrm{id},\qquad M_v=V\,\mathrm{id},\qquad M_z^b=(U^b+V^b)\,\mathrm{id}.$$ Their dependence on $A$ is algebraic on a nonempty $G$-stable open subset of $\mathop{\mathrm{Pic}}^0(C)$. Under transport on this family, $$\label{eq:matrix-transport} M_{g_*a}(U,V) =(g_{\mathrm{coeff}}M_a)(\chi(g)U,\psi(g)V).$$ Here $g_{\mathrm{coeff}}$ transports the endomorphism coefficients; scalar choices in identifying transported line bundles have no effect.* 4. *The action of $G$ on $\mathop{\mathrm{Pic}}^1(C)$ is free. For every character $\xi:G\to\mu_b$, there is a continuous map $\mathop{\mathrm{Pic}}^1(C)\to S^1$ equivariant for the given $G$-action and multiplication by $\xi$ on $S^1$.
  - Explanation: There are curves with a free-quotient cover and a linearized line bundle whose sections satisfy the binary power addition relation. Multiplication by them is given by commuting linear matrix polynomials, and the group acts freely on the degree-one Picard variety.
- **claim-binary-cover** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-binary-cover)): There are curves, a cover, and a linearized line bundle satisfying parts <span class="upright">(i)</span> and <span class="upright">(ii)</span> of Proposition <a href="#prop:binary-curves" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:binary-curves">13</a>.
  - Explanation: The curve is obtained as a normalized fiber product with an auxiliary cyclic cover that cancels ramification, giving the genus, the degree and the sections.
- **claim-binary-matrices** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-binary-matrices)): For the curve and line bundle of Lemma <a href="#lem:binary-cover" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:binary-cover">14</a>, part <span class="upright">(iii)</span> of Proposition <a href="#prop:binary-curves" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:binary-curves">13</a> holds.
  - Explanation: Multiplication by sections acts on a free graded module over a polynomial ring in two variables, giving commuting linear matrix polynomials that preserve the identities among sections.
- **claim-picard-free-action** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-picard-free)): If a finite group $G$ acts freely on a smooth connected projective complex curve $C$, then its induced action on $\mathop{\mathrm{Pic}}^1(C)$ is free.
  - Explanation: A free finite group action on a curve induces a free action on the degree-one Picard variety, because a fixed class would descend and contradict its degree.
- **claim-picard-character-maps** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-picard-character-maps)): Let $q:C\to T$ be a connected étale $G$-cover of smooth connected projective complex curves. For every character $\xi:G\to\mu_b$, there is a continuous map $$f_\xi:\mathop{\mathrm{Pic}}^1(C)\longrightarrow S^1,\qquad f_\xi(g_*A)=\xi(g)f_\xi(A).$$
  - Explanation: For each character there is a continuous circle-valued map on the degree-one Picard variety twisted by that character, built from harmonic one-forms.
- **claim-division-algebra** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-division)): Let $k$ be a finitely generated extension of the original field $\mathbb C$, and let $\Delta_0$ be a central division algebra over $k$ of degree $e_0$. Fix an integer $b\geq2$, a $k$-vector space $U$ of dimension at least three, and a subspace $$W\subseteq\mathop{\mathrm{Sym}}^b(U^\vee),\qquad s=\dim_k W,$$ such that every nonzero member of $W_{\bar k}$ has an irreducible factor of multiplicity one. Choose integers $h,M$ in this order, with $$\label{eq:division-parameters} h\geq \max\bigl(\{2\}\cup \{\,v_p(e_0)s(p^{v_p(b)}-1):p\mid e_0\,\}\bigr), \qquad M\geq2h+4s+10.$$ Here $v_p$ is the $p$-adic valuation, and the prime-indexed set is empty if $e_0=1$. Define $$Y=\left\{(\ell_1,\ldots,\ell_M)\in U^{\oplus M}: \sum_{m=1}^M w(\ell_m)=0\quad(w\in W)\right\}, \qquad X=\mathbb P(Y).$$* *Take the curve $C$, group $G$, and linearized line bundle $L$ of Proposition <a href="#prop:binary-curves" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:binary-curves">13</a> for this $b$. Write $e=\|G\|=b^b$ and $g_T=b-1$, and put $$B=\prod_{m=2}^M\mathop{\mathrm{Pic}}^0(C),\qquad H=G^{M-1},\qquad e_1=\|H\|.$$ All products and curves here are over $k$, and $H$ acts on the corresponding Picard factors by transport from the curve. Choose arbitrary characters $\rho_1,\ldots,\rho_M:H\to\mu_b$, and let $H$ act on the points of the $m$-th block of $Y$ by $\rho_m$. Let $R$ be an affine space with a faithful linear $H$-action. Set $$K=k(B\times X\times R),\qquad F=K^H.$$ For the generic tuple of degree-zero line bundles $A_m$, let $$E=\bigotimes_{m=2}^M H^0(C_K,A_m\otimes L^{g_T}).$$ Transport induces a semilinear $H$-action on $\mathop{\mathrm{End}}_K(E)$. Its fixed algebra $\Delta_1=\mathop{\mathrm{End}}_K(E)^H$ is central simple of degree $e_1$ over $F$, and $$\label{eq:division-algebra} \Delta=(\Delta_0\otimes_k F)\otimes_F\Delta_1$$ is a central division algebra of degree $e_0e_1$. Its scalar extension to $K$ identifies with $\Delta_0\otimes_k\mathop{\mathrm{End}}_K(E)$; in the corresponding descent action, $H$ fixes $\Delta_0$ pointwise.
  - Explanation: Galois descent of the endomorphism algebra of the section spaces yields a central simple algebra, and the composite algebra with the old coefficient algebra is a central division algebra of multiplied degree.
- **claim-rank-to-euler** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-rank-to-euler)): For the data of Theorem <a href="#thm:division" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:division">18</a> and a right $\Delta$-module of reduced rank $r$, there is a class $\beta$ of $H$-equivariant coherent sheaves on $Z\times X$, with a commuting right $\Delta_0$-action, having the following properties.* *After any embedding $k\hookrightarrow\mathbb C$, identify $\Delta_0\otimes_k\mathbb C$ with $\operatorname{Mat}_{e_0}(\mathbb C)$, choose a primitive matrix idempotent $\epsilon$, and put $\beta'=\beta\epsilon$. The class $\beta'$ is still $H$-equivariant and is perfect on $Z\times X_{\mathrm{sm}}$. For every $x\in X_{\mathrm{sm}}(\mathbb C)$, $$\label{eq:descent-fiber-index} \int_Z\mathop{\mathrm{ch}}\bigl(\beta'\|_{Z\times x}\bigr)\mathop{\mathrm{td}}(Z)=\pm r.$$ The sign depends only on the curve factors, not on the module or on $x$. Before making the complex base change, one has $$\label{eq:descent-twist-divisibility} p(l):=\frac{\chi(Z\times X,\beta\otimes\mathcal O_X(l))}{e_0} \ \in\ e_0e_1\mathbb Z\qquad(l\in\mathbb Z).$$ After complex base change, $p(l)$ is also the ordinary Euler characteristic of $\beta'\otimes\mathcal O_X(l)$.
  - Explanation: The reduced rank of a module is recovered up to sign as a fiber Euler characteristic, while twisted Euler characteristics of the equivariant class satisfy an arithmetic divisibility.
- **claim-twisted-extension** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-twisted-extension)): The generic module $\mathcal F_\eta$ extends to a coherent $\Delta_0$-module $\mathcal F$ on $T$ with compatible isomorphisms $J_g\otimes g_*\mathcal F\simeq\mathcal F$.
  - Explanation: The generic module extends to a coherent module over the whole parameter space with compatible corrected transport isomorphisms.
- **claim-determinant-descent** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-determinant-descent)): The sheaf $\mathcal F\otimes\mathcal D$ on $Z\times X\times R$ has a genuine $H$-equivariant structure commuting with $\Delta_0$. Its derived restriction to $R=0$ defines an equivariant coherent class $\beta$ on $Z\times X$. Nonequivariantly this class is the tensor product of $\mathcal D$ with a class pulled back from $B\times X$.
  - Explanation: Tensoring with the determinant correction cancels the scalar ambiguity of transport, giving a genuine equivariant structure and an equivariant class after restriction.
- **claim-mixed-picard-pairing** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-mixed-picard-pairing)): For any class $\gamma\in K^0(B)$, $$\int_Z\mathop{\mathrm{ch}}(\operatorname{pr}_B^*\gamma\otimes\mathcal D)\mathop{\mathrm{td}}(Z) =\pm\mathop{\mathrm{rk}}\gamma,$$ where the sign is independent of $\gamma$.
  - Explanation: Integrating the Chern character of a pulled-back class tensored with the determinant correction picks out the rank, up to a sign independent of the class.
- **claim-torsor-euler** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-torsor-euler)): Let $Q$ be a projective scheme over a field, and let $\delta:T'\to Q$ be a torsor under a constant finite group of order $a$. For every coherent-sheaf class $\alpha$ on $Q$, $$\chi(T',\delta^*\alpha)=a\,\chi(Q,\alpha).$$
  - Explanation: Pulling back a coherent class along a finite torsor multiplies its Euler characteristic by the group order.
- **claim-index-equivariant-map** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-index-equivariant-map)): There is an $H'$-equivariant map $$\Phi:\mathbb T\times\mathbb P^h(\mathbb C)\longrightarrow X_{\mathrm{sm}},$$ where $H'$ acts by translation on $\mathbb T$ and trivially on $\mathbb P^h(\mathbb C)$. It has an $H'\times S^1$-equivariant lift $$\widetilde\Phi:\mathbb T\times S^{2h+1}\longrightarrow Y_1.$$ If $t_0\in\mathbb T$ is fixed and $\Phi_0(z)=\Phi(t_0,z)$, then $\Phi$ is homotopic, as an ordinary map, to $\Phi_0$ composed with projection onto $\mathbb P^h(\mathbb C)$, and $$\Phi_0^*\mathcal O_X(-1)\simeq\mathcal O_{\mathbb P^h}(-1).$$ There is also an $H$-equivariant map $\vartheta:P\longrightarrow\mathbb T$, for the action through $\rho$.
  - Explanation: High connectivity of the unit cone lets one extend sections cell by cell, producing equivariant maps from a torus times projective space into the smooth locus, with an equivariant lift.
- **claim-index-integral-pushforward** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-index-integral-pushforward)): Let $V$ be a smooth complex projective variety and let $\xi\in K^0(V\times\mathbb P^h(\mathbb C))$. There is a unique $I_V(\xi)\in K^0(\mathbb P^h(\mathbb C))$ such that $$\label{eq:index-integral-pushforward} \mathop{\mathrm{ch}}(I_V(\xi)) =\int_V\mathop{\mathrm{ch}}(\xi)\mathop{\mathrm{td}}(T_V).$$ The integral on the right is integration over the $V$-factor.
  - Explanation: A topological K-theory pushforward exists whose Chern character is the fiber integral against the Todd class, so integrality is retained.
- **claim-index-test** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-index-test)): In the setting of Theorem <a href="#thm:division" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:division">18</a>, let $r$ be the reduced rank of any finite right $\Delta$-module. There is a smooth $h$-dimensional linear section $D_0\subset X_{\mathrm{sm}}$, a complete intersection of $s$ hypersurfaces of degree $b$ in $\mathbb P^{h+s}(\mathbb C)$, and integers $q_0,\ldots,q_h\in e_1\mathbb Z$ such that $q_0=\pm r$ and $$\label{eq:index-section-divisibility} \sum_{j=0}^h q_j\, \chi\!\left(D_0, (1-[\mathcal O_{D_0}(-1)])^j\otimes\mathcal O_{D_0}(l)\right) \in e_0e_1\mathbb Z \qquad(l\in\mathbb Z).$$
  - Explanation: On a smooth linear section of the parameter variety, an integral combination of twisted Euler characteristics with leading coefficient equal to the reduced rank up to sign is divisible by the product of the two degrees.
- **claim-padic-truncation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-index-padic-truncation)): Let $p$ be a prime, let $v\ge1$, and let $A,Q\in\mathbb Z[u]$. Suppose the reduction of $A$ modulo $p$ has exact order $\nu\ge0$ at $u=0$. If $h\ge v\nu$ and $$A(u)Q(u)=0\pmod{(p^v,u^{h+1})},$$ then $p^v\mid Q(0)$.
  - Explanation: A congruence modulo a prime power and a high power of the variable forces the constant coefficient to be divisible by that prime power, when the degree is large enough.
- **claim-veronese-extension** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-veronese-extension)): Let $b\ge2$, let $k$ contain a primitive $b$-th root of unity $\zeta$, and let $A$ be a commutative $k$-algebra with elements $u,v$. Introduce symbols $T_\alpha$, indexed by $\alpha\in\mathbb Z_{\ge0}^{b}$ with $\|\alpha\|=b$, and put $$D_j=T_{b e_j}\quad(0\le j<b),\qquad z=\frac1b\sum_{j=0}^{b-1}D_j,\qquad u_T=-\frac1b\sum_{j=0}^{b-1}\zeta^{-j}D_j,\qquad v_T=T_{(1,\ldots,1)}.$$ Let $A'$ be the quotient of $A[T_\alpha]$ by the relations $$\begin{aligned} T_\alpha T_\beta&=T_\gamma T_\delta &&\text{whenever }\alpha+\beta=\gamma+\delta, \label{eq:veronese-exchanges}\\ D_j&=z-\zeta^j u_T &&(0\le j<b),\qquad u_T=u,\quad v_T=v. \label{eq:veronese-linear} \end{aligned}$$ Then $A'$ is finite over $A$, and in $A'$ $$z^b=u^b+v^b,\qquad T_\alpha^b=\prod_{j=0}^{b-1}D_j^{\alpha_j}. \label{eq:veronese-monic}$$ If $A$ is graded and $u,v$ have degree one, these are homogeneous relations when each $T_\alpha$ has degree one; the defining relations <a href="#eq:veronese-exchanges" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:veronese-exchanges">[eq:veronese-exchanges]</a>–<a href="#eq:veronese-linear" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:veronese-linear">[eq:veronese-linear]</a> have degrees two and one.
  - Explanation: Linear and quadratic relations among symbols indexed by monomials define a finite extension in which binary power addition holds.
- **claim-induction-step** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-induction-step)): Fix $1\le i\le n$. Suppose that a field $k$, a central division algebra $\Delta_0$ over $k$, a formal space $U$, and a low-relation ideal $Q\subseteq\mathop{\mathrm{Sym}}_k U$ satisfy the induction conditions of Section <a href="#sec:low-relations" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:low-relations">3</a>, with the triangular identities already constructed for the rows $d>i$. Then there are a finitely generated extension $F/k$, a central division algebra $\Delta$ over $F$, and an enlarged formal space and low-relation ideal over $F$ satisfying the same conditions with the rows $d\ge i$ constructed. In particular, the enlarged formal space contains a form $t_i$ for which $$t_i^{a_i} =c_i f_i+\sum_{l<i}B_{il}f_l+\sum_{d>i}C_{id}t_d \pmod{Q_{\mathrm{new}}}, \qquad c_i\in F^\times. \label{eq:new-triangular-row}$$ All the enlarged formal forms evaluate to mutually commuting linear forms in $\Delta[x]$, and the new relation ideal is generated by linear forms and quadrics.
  - Explanation: One induction step adds a new commuting linear form with a triangular power identity over a larger central division algebra, while keeping all earlier identities and a relation ideal generated by linear forms and quadrics.
- **claim-local-cohomology-extremality** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-local-cohomology)): Let $F$ be a field of characteristic zero, let $S_F=F[x_1,\ldots,x_n]$ be standard graded, and put $\mathfrak m=(x_1,\ldots,x_n)$. Let $$1\le c\le n,\qquad 2\le a_1\le\cdots\le a_c, \qquad P_F=(x_1^{a_1},\ldots,x_c^{a_c}).$$ Suppose that a homogeneous ideal $I\subseteq S_F$ contains a homogeneous regular sequence $f_1,\ldots,f_c$ with $\deg f_k=a_k$. Let $J$ be the lex-plus-powers ideal prescribed by Corollary <a href="#cor:characteristic-zero" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cor:characteristic-zero">3</a> for $P_F$, the order $x_1>\cdots>x_n$, and the Hilbert function of $S_F/I$. For this single ideal, $$\dim_F H^i_{\mathfrak m}(S_F/I)_j \le \dim_F H^i_{\mathfrak m}(S_F/J)_j \qquad(i\ge0,\ j\in\mathbb Z).$$
  - Explanation: The lex-plus-powers ideal bounds the local cohomology dimensions of the quotient by any homogeneous ideal containing the regular sequence.
- **claim-quadratic-cayley-bacharach** (bound, [source](#stmt-cor-quadratic-cayley-bacharach)): Let $F$ have characteristic zero, let $r\ge1$, and let $\Omega\subseteq\mathbb P^r_F$ be a zero-dimensional scheme-theoretic complete intersection of $r$ quadrics, of degree $2^r$. For an integer $1\le k\le r$ and a degree-$k$ hypersurface $X\subseteq\mathbb P^r_F$, $$\Omega\not\subseteq X \quad\Longrightarrow\quad \deg(\Omega\cap X)\le 2^r-2^{r-k}.$$ Equivalently, if $X$ contains a closed subscheme of $\Omega$ of degree strictly greater than $2^r-2^{r-k}$, then it contains $\Omega$ scheme-theoretically. Degrees here mean scheme lengths over $F$; no reducedness assumption is needed.
  - Explanation: A hypersurface not containing a zero-dimensional complete intersection of quadrics meets it in a length bounded by the stated difference of powers.

        "exact_quote": "Let $F$ be a field of characteristic zero, let $S_F=F[x_1,\\ldots,x_n]$ be standard graded, and let $$1\\le c\\le n,\\qquad 2\\le a_1\\le\\cdots\\le a_c.$$ If a homogeneous ideal $I\\subseteq S_F$ contains a homogeneous regular sequence $f_1,\\ldots,f_c$ with $\\deg f_i=a_i$, there is a monomial ideal $J\\subseteq S_F$ containing $P_F=(x_1^{a_1},\\ldots,x_c^{a_c})$ such that $$\\dim_F(S_F/J)_d=\\dim_F(S_F/I)_d\\qquad(d\\ge0).$$ Moreover, $J$ may be chosen lex-plus-powers: in each degree its monomials outside $P_F$ are the lexicographically greatest $\\dim_F I_d-\\dim_F(P_F)_d$ monomials outside $P_F$, for the order $x_1>\\cdots>x_n$.",
        "source_id": "PM-200.2"
      },

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-ordered-basis",
        "exact_quote": "Let $f_1,\\ldots,f_n$ be a homogeneous regular sequence in $S=\\mathbb C[x_1,\\ldots,x_n]$, with $\\deg f_i=a_i>0$. Suppose that a field extension $k/\\mathbb C$ and a finite-dimensional central division $k$-algebra $\\Delta$ have the following property. There is a finite collection of pairwise commuting elements of $\\Delta[x_1,\\ldots,x_n]_1$, containing $x_1,\\ldots,x_n$, whose generated commutative $k$-algebra $A$ contains elements $t_1,\\ldots,t_n$ of degree one satisfying $$\\label{eq:triangular}\n t_i^{a_i}\n =c_i f_i+\\sum_{l<i}B_{il}f_l+\\sum_{d>i}C_{id}t_d,\n \\qquad 1\\le i\\le n,$$ where $c_i\\in k^\\times$ and all terms are homogeneous, with $B_{il},C_{id}\\in A$. Then $A$ is finite over $k[x_1,\\ldots,x_n]$, and $$\\{t_1^{\\alpha_1}\\cdots t_n^{\\alpha_n}:\\alpha\\in\\mathbb Z_{\\ge0}^n\\}$$ is a basis of $\\Delta[x_1,\\ldots,x_n]$ as a left $\\Delta$-vector space.",
        "source_id": "PM-200.2"
      },
      "evidence": [

Then $A$ is finite over $k[x_1,\\ldots,x_n]$, and $$\\{t_1^{\\alpha_1}\\cdots t_n^{\\alpha_n}:\\alpha\\in\\mathbb Z_{\\ge0}^n\\}$$ is a basis of $\\Delta[x_1,\\ldots,x_n]$ as a left $\\Delta$-vector space.",

      },

          "source_id": "PM-200.2"
        }
      ],

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-homogeneous-joins",
        "exact_quote": "Let $w$ be a nonzero homogeneous polynomial of degree $b\\ge2$ on a complex vector space $U$. Suppose that some irreducible factor of $w$ has multiplicity one. Consequently, $$\\widetilde H_j(F_w;\\mathbb Z)=0\\qquad(0\\le j\\le2M-2).$$",
        "source_id": "PM-200.2"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-thm-parameters",
        "exact_quote": "Let $k$ be a field admitting an embedding into $\\mathbb C$, let $U$ be a finite-dimensional $k$-vector space with $\\dim U\\ge3$, and let $$W\\subseteq\\mathop{\\mathrm{Sym}}^b(U^\\vee),\\qquad b\\ge2,\\qquad s=\\dim W.$$ Assume that every nonzero element of $W_{\\bar k}$ has an irreducible factor of multiplicity one over an algebraic closure $\\bar k$. For integers $h\\ge2$ and $M\\ge2h+4s+10$, put $N_*=M\\dim U$ and define $$\\begin{split}\n Y&=\\left\\{(\\ell_1,\\ldots,\\ell_M)\\in U^{\\oplus M}:\n                 \\sum_{m=1}^M w(\\ell_m)=0\\quad(w\\in W)\\right\\},\\\\\n X&=\\mathbb P(Y)\\subseteq\\mathbb P(U^{\\oplus M}).\n \\end{split}$$ Then $X$ is a geometrically integral complete intersection of codimension $s$, cut out by $s$ equations of degree $b$, and $$\\operatorname{codim}_X\\operatorname{Sing}X\n       \\ge M-2s+1>h,$$ with the codimension of the empty locus understood to be infinite. In particular, a general $h$-dimensional projective linear section is a smooth complete intersection contained in [(eq:parameter-jacobian)](#eq:parameter-jacobian)$X_{\\mathrm{sm}}$.*\n\n*After any embedding $k\\hookrightarrow\\mathbb C$, let $Y^o$ be the nonzero cone over $X_{\\mathrm{sm}}$. Both $Y^o$ and its unit-norm retract are $(M+2h)$-connected.",
        "source_id": "PM-200.2"
      },
      "evidence": [

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-binary-curves",
        "exact_quote": "Let $b\\ge2$, fix a primitive $b$-th root $\\zeta$, and put $$G=(\\mu_b^b/\\mu_b)\\rtimes(\\mathbb Z/b),\\qquad e=b^b,$$ where the denominator is the diagonal subgroup and $\\mathbb Z/b$ permutes the $b$ factors cyclically. There are smooth connected projective curves $C,T_0$, a connected étale $G$-cover $C\\to T_0$, and a genuinely $G$-linearized line bundle $L$ on $C$ with the following properties.*\n\n1.  *The genera and degree satisfy $$g_T:=g(T_0)=b-1,\\qquad g(C)-1=e(g_T-1),\\qquad \\deg L=e.$$*\n\n\n2.  *There are sections $a_\\alpha\\in H^0(C,L)$, indexed by $\\alpha\\in\\mathbb Z_{\\ge0}^b$ with $|\\alpha|=b$, whose transport follows the degree-$b$ monomial representation in formal homogeneous coordinates $p_0,\\ldots,p_{b-1}$. The notation $p^\\alpha$ labels the section $a_\\alpha$ when $|\\alpha|=b$; it does not assign individual sections of $L$ to the $p_j$. Write $a_\\alpha=p^\\alpha$ and set $$z=\\frac1b\\sum_{j=0}^{b-1}p_j^b,\\qquad\n     u=-\\frac1b\\sum_{j=0}^{b-1}\\zeta^{-j}p_j^b,\\qquad\n     v=p_0\\cdots p_{b-1}.$$ They satisfy $$\\label{eq:binary-section-relations}\n     p_j^b=z-\\zeta^ju,\\qquad z^b=u^b+v^b.$$ The section $z$ is invariant, and $u,v$ have characters $\\chi,\\psi:G\\to\\mu_b$, respectively. The pencil $u,v$ is base-point-free and defines a degree-$e$ morphism $\\pi:C\\to\\mathbb P^1$ with $L=\\pi^*\\mathcal O(1)$.*\n\n\n\n3.  *For a generic $A\\in\\mathop{\\mathrm{Pic}}^0(C)$, over its field of definition $\\kappa$, the space $E_A=H^0(C,A L^{g_T})$ has dimension $e$. Multiplication of sections gives isomorphisms $$\\label{eq:curve-free-module}\n     H^0(C,A L^{g_T+j})\n       =E_A\\otimes_\\kappa\\mathop{\\mathrm{Sym}}^j_\\kappa\\langle u,v\\rangle\n           \\qquad(j\\ge0).$$ Every $a\\in H^0(C,L)$ consequently acts by a homogeneous linear matrix polynomial $M_a(U,V)\\in\\mathop{\\mathrm{End}}_\\kappa(E_A)[U,V]$. These matrix polynomials commute, preserve all polynomial identities among the sections, and satisfy $$M_u=U\\,\\mathrm{id},\\qquad M_v=V\\,\\mathrm{id},\\qquad\n     M_z^b=(U^b+V^b)\\,\\mathrm{id}.$$ Their dependence on $A$ is algebraic on a nonempty $G$-stable open subset of $\\mathop{\\mathrm{Pic}}^0(C)$. Under transport on this family, $$\\label{eq:matrix-transport}\n     M_{g_*a}(U,V)\n         =(g_{\\mathrm{coeff}}M_a)(\\chi(g)U,\\psi(g)V).$$ Here $g_{\\mathrm{coeff}}$ transports the endomorphism coefficients; scalar choices in identifying transported line bundles have no effect.*\n\n4.  *The action of $G$ on $\\mathop{\\mathrm{Pic}}^1(C)$ is free. For every character $\\xi:G\\to\\mu_b$, there is a continuous map $\\mathop{\\mathrm{Pic}}^1(C)\\to S^1$ equivariant for the given $G$-action and multiplication by $\\xi$ on $S^1$.",
        "source_id": "PM-200.2"
      },

There are smooth connected projective curves $C,T_0$, a connected étale $G$-cover $C\\to T_0$, and a genuinely $G$-linearized line bundle $L$ on $C$ with the following properties.*\n\n1.  *The genera and degree satisfy $$g_T:=g(T_0)=b-1,\\qquad g(C)-1=e(g_T-1),\\qquad \\deg L=e.$$*\n\n\n2.  *There are sections $a_\\alpha\\in H^0(C,L)$, indexed by $\\alpha\\in\\mathbb Z_{\\ge0}^b$ with $|\\alpha|=b$, whose transport follows the degree-$b$ monomial representation in formal homogeneous coordinates $p_0,\\ldots,p_{b-1}$. For every character $\\xi:G\\to\\mu_b$, there is a continuous map $\\mathop{\\mathrm{Pic}}^1(C)\\to S^1$ equivariant for the given $G$-action and multiplication by $\\xi$ on $S^1$.",
      "explanation": "There are curves with a free-quotient cover and a linearized line bundle whose sections satisfy the binary power addition relation. Multiplication by them is given by commuting linear matrix polynomials, and the group acts freely on the degree-one Picard variety.",

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-picard-character-maps",
        "exact_quote": "Let $q:C\\to T$ be a connected étale $G$-cover of smooth connected projective complex curves. For every character $\\xi:G\\to\\mu_b$, there is a continuous map $$f_\\xi:\\mathop{\\mathrm{Pic}}^1(C)\\longrightarrow S^1,\\qquad\n f_\\xi(g_*A)=\\xi(g)f_\\xi(A).$$",

        }

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-thm-division",
        "exact_quote": "Let $k$ be a finitely generated extension of the original field $\\mathbb C$, and let $\\Delta_0$ be a central division algebra over $k$ of degree $e_0$. Fix an integer $b\\geq2$, a $k$-vector space $U$ of dimension at least three, and a subspace $$W\\subseteq\\mathop{\\mathrm{Sym}}^b(U^\\vee),\\qquad s=\\dim_k W,$$ such that every nonzero member of $W_{\\bar k}$ has an irreducible factor of multiplicity one. Choose integers $h,M$ in this order, with $$\\label{eq:division-parameters}\nh\\geq\n\\max\\bigl(\\{2\\}\\cup\n\\{\\,v_p(e_0)s(p^{v_p(b)}-1):p\\mid e_0\\,\\}\\bigr),\n\\qquad M\\geq2h+4s+10.$$ Here $v_p$ is the $p$-adic valuation, and the prime-indexed set is empty if $e_0=1$. Define $$Y=\\left\\{(\\ell_1,\\ldots,\\ell_M)\\in U^{\\oplus M}:\n          \\sum_{m=1}^M w(\\ell_m)=0\\quad(w\\in W)\\right\\},\n\\qquad X=\\mathbb P(Y).$$*\n\n*Take the curve $C$, group $G$, and linearized line bundle $L$ of Proposition <a href=\"#prop:binary-curves\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:binary-curves\">13</a> for this $b$. Write $e=|G|=b^b$ and $g_T=b-1$, and put $$B=\\prod_{m=2}^M\\mathop{\\mathrm{Pic}}^0(C),\\qquad\nH=G^{M-1},\\qquad e_1=|H|.$$ All products and curves here are over $k$, and $H$ acts on the corresponding Picard factors by transport from the curve. Choose arbitrary characters $\\rho_1,\\ldots,\\rho_M:H\\to\\mu_b$, and let $H$ act on the points of the $m$-th block of $Y$ by $\\rho_m$. Let $R$ be an affine space with a faithful linear $H$-action. Set $$K=k(B\\times X\\times R),\\qquad F=K^H.$$ For the generic tuple of degree-zero line bundles $A_m$, let $$E=\\bigotimes_{m=2}^M H^0(C_K,A_m\\otimes L^{g_T}).$$ Transport induces a semilinear $H$-action on $\\mathop{\\mathrm{End}}_K(E)$. Its fixed algebra $\\Delta_1=\\mathop{\\mathrm{End}}_K(E)^H$ is central simple of degree $e_1$ over $F$, and $$\\label{eq:division-algebra}\n\\Delta=(\\Delta_0\\otimes_k F)\\otimes_F\\Delta_1$$ is a central division algebra of degree $e_0e_1$. Its scalar extension to $K$ identifies with $\\Delta_0\\otimes_k\\mathop{\\mathrm{End}}_K(E)$; in the corresponding descent action, $H$ fixes $\\Delta_0$ pointwise.",

          "exact_quote": "Let $k$ be a finitely generated extension of the original field $\\mathbb C$, and let $\\Delta_0$ be a central division algebra over $k$ of degree $e_0$. Fix an integer $b\\geq2$, a $k$-vector space $U$ of dimension at least three, and a subspace $$W\\subseteq\\mathop{\\mathrm{Sym}}^b(U^\\vee),\\qquad s=\\dim_k W,$$ such that every nonzero member of $W_{\\bar k}$ has an irreducible factor of multiplicity one. Choose integers $h,M$ in this order, with $$\\label{eq:division-parameters}\nh\\geq\n\\max\\bigl(\\{2\\}\\cup\n\\{\\,v_p(e_0)s(p^{v_p(b)}-1):p\\mid e_0\\,\\}\\bigr),\n\\qquad M\\geq2h+4s+10.$$ Here $v_p$ is the $p$-adic valuation, and the prime-indexed set is empty if $e_0=1$. Define $$Y=\\left\\{(\\ell_1,\\ldots,\\ell_M)\\in U^{\\oplus M}:\n          \\sum_{m=1}^M w(\\ell_m)=0\\quad(w\\in W)\\right\\},\n\\qquad X=\\mathbb P(Y).$$*\n\n*Take the curve $C$, group $G$, and linearized line bundle $L$ of Proposition <a href=\"#prop:binary-curves\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:binary-curves\">13</a> for this $b$. Write $e=|G|=b^b$ and $g_T=b-1$, and put $$B=\\prod_{m=2}^M\\mathop{\\mathrm{Pic}}^0(C),\\qquad\nH=G^{M-1},\\qquad e_1=|H|.$$ All products and curves here are over $k$, and $H$ acts on the corresponding Picard factors by transport from the curve.         "anchor": "stmt-prop-rank-to-euler",
        "exact_quote": "For the data of Theorem <a href=\"#thm:division\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:division\">18</a> and a right $\\Delta$-module of reduced rank $r$, there is a class $\\beta$ of $H$-equivariant coherent sheaves on $Z\\times X$, with a commuting right $\\Delta_0$-action, having the following properties.*\n\n\n\n*After any embedding $k\\hookrightarrow\\mathbb C$, identify $\\Delta_0\\otimes_k\\mathbb C$ with $\\operatorname{Mat}_{e_0}(\\mathbb C)$, choose a primitive matrix idempotent $\\epsilon$, and put $\\beta'=\\beta\\epsilon$. The class $\\beta'$ is still $H$-equivariant and is perfect on $Z\\times X_{\\mathrm{sm}}$. For every $x\\in X_{\\mathrm{sm}}(\\mathbb C)$, $$\\label{eq:descent-fiber-index}\n\\int_Z\\mathop{\\mathrm{ch}}\\bigl(\\beta'|_{Z\\times x}\\bigr)\\mathop{\\mathrm{td}}(Z)=\\pm r.$$ The sign depends only on the curve factors, not on the module or on $x$. Before making the complex base change, one has $$\\label{eq:descent-twist-divisibility}\np(l):=\\frac{\\chi(Z\\times X,\\beta\\otimes\\mathcal O_X(l))}{e_0}\n       \\ \\in\\ e_0e_1\\mathbb Z\\qquad(l\\in\\mathbb Z).$$ After complex base change, $p(l)$ is also the ordinary Euler characteristic of $\\beta'\\otimes\\mathcal O_X(l)$.",
        "source_id": "PM-200.2"
      },

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-index-equivariant-map",
        "exact_quote": "There is an $H'$-equivariant map $$\\Phi:\\mathbb T\\times\\mathbb P^h(\\mathbb C)\\longrightarrow X_{\\mathrm{sm}},$$ where $H'$ acts by translation on $\\mathbb T$ and trivially on $\\mathbb P^h(\\mathbb C)$. It has an $H'\\times S^1$-equivariant lift $$\\widetilde\\Phi:\\mathbb T\\times S^{2h+1}\\longrightarrow Y_1.$$ If $t_0\\in\\mathbb T$ is fixed and $\\Phi_0(z)=\\Phi(t_0,z)$, then $\\Phi$ is homotopic, as an ordinary map, to $\\Phi_0$ composed with projection onto $\\mathbb P^h(\\mathbb C)$, and $$\\Phi_0^*\\mathcal O_X(-1)\\simeq\\mathcal O_{\\mathbb P^h}(-1).$$ There is also an $H$-equivariant map $\\vartheta:P\\longrightarrow\\mathbb T$, for the action through $\\rho$.",
        "source_id": "PM-200.2"
      },
      "evidence": [

It has an $H'\\times S^1$-equivariant lift $$\\widetilde\\Phi:\\mathbb T\\times S^{2h+1}\\longrightarrow Y_1.$$ If $t_0\\in\\mathbb T$ is fixed and $\\Phi_0(z)=\\Phi(t_0,z)$, then $\\Phi$ is homotopic, as an ordinary map, to $\\Phi_0$ composed with projection onto $\\mathbb P^h(\\mathbb C)$, and $$\\Phi_0^*\\mathcal O_X(-1)\\simeq\\mathcal O_{\\mathbb P^h}(-1).$$ There is also an $H$-equivariant map $\\vartheta:P\\longrightarrow\\mathbb T$, for the action through $\\rho$.",
          "source_id": "PM-200.2"
        }

      "exact_statement": "There is an $H'$-equivariant map $$\\Phi:\\mathbb T\\times\\mathbb P^h(\\mathbb C)\\longrightarrow X_{\\mathrm{sm}},$$ where $H'$ acts by translation on $\\mathbb T$ and trivially on $\\mathbb P^h(\\mathbb C)$.         "anchor": "stmt-prop-index-test",
        "exact_quote": "In the setting of Theorem <a href=\"#thm:division\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:division\">18</a>, let $r$ be the reduced rank of any finite right $\\Delta$-module. There is a smooth $h$-dimensional linear section $D_0\\subset X_{\\mathrm{sm}}$, a complete intersection of $s$ hypersurfaces of degree $b$ in $\\mathbb P^{h+s}(\\mathbb C)$, and integers $q_0,\\ldots,q_h\\in e_1\\mathbb Z$ such that $q_0=\\pm r$ and $$\\label{eq:index-section-divisibility}\n \\sum_{j=0}^h q_j\\,\n \\chi\\!\\left(D_0,\n       (1-[\\mathcal O_{D_0}(-1)])^j\\otimes\\mathcal O_{D_0}(l)\\right)\n       \\in e_0e_1\\mathbb Z\n       \\qquad(l\\in\\mathbb Z).$$",
        "source_id": "PM-200.2"
      },
      "evidence": [

        "exact_quote": "Let $b\\ge2$, let $k$ contain a primitive $b$-th root of unity $\\zeta$, and let $A$ be a commutative $k$-algebra with elements $u,v$. Introduce symbols $T_\\alpha$, indexed by $\\alpha\\in\\mathbb Z_{\\ge0}^{b}$ with $|\\alpha|=b$, and put $$D_j=T_{b e_j}\\quad(0\\le j<b),\\qquad\n z=\\frac1b\\sum_{j=0}^{b-1}D_j,\\qquad\n u_T=-\\frac1b\\sum_{j=0}^{b-1}\\zeta^{-j}D_j,\\qquad\n v_T=T_{(1,\\ldots,1)}.$$ Let $A'$ be the quotient of $A[T_\\alpha]$ by the relations $$\\begin{aligned}\n T_\\alpha T_\\beta&=T_\\gamma T_\\delta\n       &&\\text{whenever }\\alpha+\\beta=\\gamma+\\delta,\n       \\label{eq:veronese-exchanges}\\\\\n D_j&=z-\\zeta^j u_T\n       &&(0\\le j<b),\\qquad u_T=u,\\quad v_T=v.\n       \\label{eq:veronese-linear}\n\\end{aligned}$$ Then $A'$ is finite over $A$, and in $A'$ $$z^b=u^b+v^b,\\qquad\n T_\\alpha^b=\\prod_{j=0}^{b-1}D_j^{\\alpha_j}.\n \\label{eq:veronese-monic}$$ If $A$ is graded and $u,v$ have degree one, these are homogeneous relations when each $T_\\alpha$ has degree one; the defining relations <a href=\"#eq:veronese-exchanges\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:veronese-exchanges\">[eq:veronese-exchanges]</a>–<a href=\"#eq:veronese-linear\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:veronese-linear\">[eq:veronese-linear]</a> have degrees two and one.",
        "source_id": "PM-200.2"
      },
      "evidence": [

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **regular sequence** ([source](#sec:introduction)): A sequence $f_1,\ldots,f_n$ of positive-degree homogeneous elements is *regular* if multiplication by $f_i$ is injective on $S/(f_1,\ldots,f_{i-1})$ for each $i$.
- **polynomial algebra over a division algebra** ([source](#sec:introduction)): If $k/\mathbb C$ is a field extension and $\Delta$ is a central division $k$-algebra, then $\Delta[x_1,\ldots,x_n]$ denotes the polynomial algebra with central variables $x_j$.
- **degree of a central simple algebra** ([source](#subsec:conventions)): The degree of a central simple algebra is the square root of its dimension over its center.
- **characteristic set** ([source](#pm-h-8)): We call $\mathcal C(Q)$ the characteristic set of $Q$.

| q-block-count | number of blocks M | ≥ | $2h+4s+10$ | integers h and M with the stated lower bounds, s the dimension of W |

| q-genus-base-curve | genus of the base curve | = | $b-1$ | b at least two |
| q-group-order | order of the group and degree of the line bundle | = | $b^b$ | b at least two |
| q-curve-field-transcendence | transcendence degree bound of a field of definition of a curve | = | $\binom{n-1+D}{D}^2$ | degree at most D |
| q-curve-degree-bound | excluded curve degree D | = | $\bigl(\max_{1\le j\le n}a_j\bigr)^2$ | largest degree of the regular sequence |

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **s-ordered-basis** [claim](#stmt-lem-ordered-basis) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-ordered-basis): System-of-parameters argument on a finite Cohen-Macaulay module plus a Hilbert series count -> ordered left basis of the polynomial algebra
- **s-monomial-extraction** [claim](#stmt-prop-monomial-reduction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-monomial-reduction): Least exponents in the ordered basis form an upward-closed set -> monomial ideal with equal Hilbert function
- **s-basepoint** [claim](#stmt-lem-stage-basepoint) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-stage-basepoint): Common zero propagates through triangular rows -> base-point-freeness
- **s-simple-factor** [claim](#stmt-lem-simple-factor) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-simple-factor): Gradient map argument against forbidden curves -> simple factor of every annihilating polynomial
- **s-curve-field** [claim](#stmt-lem-bounded-curve-field) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-bounded-curve-field): Chow form incidence equation over a bounded coefficient field -> bounded field of definition
- **s-initial-data** [claim](#stmt-prop-initial-data) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-initial-data): Skew Laurent algebra with generic quadrics and square-class independence -> initial induction data
- **s-sparsity** [claim](#stmt-lem-sparsity-preserved) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-sparsity-preserved): Finite projection pulling back the hyperplane bundle -> inheritance of curve exclusion
- **s-joins** [claim](#stmt-lem-homogeneous-joins) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-homogeneous-joins): Open cover and homotopy pushout giving joins -> connected levels and homology vanishing
- **s-parameters** [claim](#stmt-thm-parameters) / [proof](#proof-stmt-thm-parameters): Jacobian dimension count, Leray spectral sequence and Lefschetz theorem -> integral complete intersection with highly connected smooth cone
- **s-binary-cover** [claim](#stmt-lem-binary-cover) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-binary-cover): Kummer theory and normalized fiber product with a cyclic cover -> curve, etale cover and linearized line bundle
- **s-binary-matrices** [claim](#stmt-lem-binary-matrices) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-binary-matrices): Pushforward is a trivial bundle on the projective line -> commuting linear matrix polynomials
- **s-picard-free** [claim](#stmt-lem-picard-free) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-picard-free): Descent along a cyclic etale cover contradicts degree -> free action on degree-one Picard variety
- **s-picard-characters** [claim](#stmt-lem-picard-character-maps) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-picard-character-maps): Integrate invariant harmonic one-forms with integral periods -> character-equivariant circle-valued maps
- **s-binary-curves** [claim](#stmt-prop-binary-curves) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-picard-character-maps-2): Assemble the cover, matrices and Picard properties -> binary addition data
- **s-twisted-extension** [claim](#stmt-lem-twisted-extension) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-twisted-extension): Sum of coherent subsheaves under corrected transport -> coherent extension with twisted equivariance
- **s-determinant-descent** [claim](#stmt-lem-determinant-descent) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-determinant-descent): Cancel scalar weights with a determinant of cohomology line -> equivariant class
- **s-mixed-pairing** [claim](#stmt-lem-mixed-picard-pairing) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-mixed-picard-pairing): Grothendieck-Riemann-Roch and a unimodular pairing -> rank extraction up to sign
- **s-torsor-euler** [claim](#stmt-lem-torsor-euler) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-torsor-euler): Nilpotent operator on the group of coherent sheaves -> Euler characteristic multiplication
- **s-rank-to-euler** [claim](#stmt-prop-rank-to-euler) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-torsor-euler-2): Descent along the free quotient and divisibility of module dimensions -> fiber index and twist divisibility
- **s-index-map** [claim](#stmt-lem-index-equivariant-map) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-index-equivariant-map): Cell-by-cell section extension using connectivity -> equivariant maps into the smooth locus
- **s-index-pushforward** [claim](#stmt-lem-index-integral-pushforward) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-index-integral-pushforward): Thom class and integral projective bundle formula -> integral pushforward
- **s-index-test** [claim](#stmt-prop-index-test) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-index-test): Pull back along equivariant maps and compare with a smooth linear section -> divisibility of an integral combination of Euler characteristics
- **s-padic** [claim](#stmt-lem-index-padic-truncation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-index-padic-truncation): Successive truncation and division by the prime -> divisibility of the constant coefficient
- **s-division** [claim](#stmt-thm-division) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-index-padic-truncation-2): Rank divisibility excludes proper right ideals -> central division algebra
- **s-veronese** [claim](#stmt-lem-veronese-extension) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-veronese-extension): Exchange relations and integrality of the symbols -> finite extension with power addition
- **s-induction-step** [claim](#stmt-prop-induction-step) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-induction-step): Chain of binary additions with invariant sum and Galois descent -> new triangular row
- **s-construction** [claim](#stmt-thm-construction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-induction-step-2): Iterate the induction step from the last row to the first -> commuting forms with ordered basis
- **s-main** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-induction-step-3): Apply monomial extraction to the construction -> Artinian EGH over the complex numbers
- **s-char-zero** [claim](#stmt-cor-characteristic-zero) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-induction-step-4): Reduce to a full regular sequence and descend through a finitely generated field -> characteristic-zero statement with lex-plus-powers ideal
- **s-local-cohomology** [claim](#stmt-cor-local-cohomology) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-local-cohomology): Feed the characteristic-zero statement into a conditional local cohomology theorem -> local cohomology inequalities
- **s-cayley-bacharach** [claim](#stmt-cor-quadratic-cayley-bacharach) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-quadratic-cayley-bacharach): Squarefree monomial count and residual duality -> degree bound for intersections

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **inv-ordered-left-basis**: For a complex homogeneous regular sequence with degrees at least two, a finitely generated field extension, a finite-dimensional central division algebra and pairwise commuting linear forms exist. The ordered monomials in the distinguished forms t_1,...,t_n then give a left basis of the full polynomial algebra over the division algebra.
- **inv-hilbert-function-monomial-ideal**: Every homogeneous ideal containing such a regular sequence has the Hilbert function of one monomial ideal that contains the pure powers x_i^{a_i}.
- **inv-simple-factor-property**: If the geometric characteristic set of the low-relation ideal contains no integral curve of degree at most b(b-1), then every nonzero element of the annihilator space W has an irreducible factor of multiplicity one after any algebraically closed extension.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-positive-characteristic**: Apply the monomial-ideal Hilbert function comparison over a field of positive characteristic, for example in modular or finite-field polynomial solvers. -> The comparison is established only for coefficient fields of characteristic zero; nothing in the source transfers it to positive characteristic.
- **anti-linear-form-degree**: Feed a sequence containing a linear form, or a degree below two, to the commuting-form construction. -> The construction assumes every degree is at least two, so its conclusions are not available when a degree is one.
- **anti-non-regular-system**: Apply the ordered-basis criterion to an arbitrary polynomial system that is not a homogeneous regular sequence. -> The Hilbert series product formula and the zero-dimensional quotient step both rely on regularity and homogeneity, so they fail for general or inhomogeneous systems.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The main results are existence statements. The commuting-form construction passes through generic fields of definition, Galois descent, Picard varieties of curves, topological K-theory and a proof that a descended algebra is a division algebra. No finite procedure with checkable inputs and outputs is stated, and the monomial ideal is extracted from least exponents in an infinite-dimensional algebra over a function field. The source states the existence of the extension, algebra and forms, not an algorithm computing them.

<a id="pm-s5"></a>
## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The main results assert that a monomial ideal with the same Hilbert function exists. They give no executable routine, so there is no library component or API to specify. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The statements concern graded dimensions of polynomial quotients. They contain no state, dynamics or stochastic process to simulate. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The existence results are not a finite procedure, so no checker, solver or converter can be built directly from the stated theorems. |
| algorithms | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The result gives existence of J and of a commuting-form construction. The construction goes through field extensions and geometric genericity choices, and the statements give neither a complexity bound nor a finite step list. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No representation or invariant for a data structure is proposed. Monomial ideals are the mathematical output object, not an engineered structure with operations. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No arithmetic kernel or parallel dataflow is derived. The division-algebra and sheaf-theoretic steps are not mapped to a circuit. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No hardness assumption, protocol or security reduction is stated. Ideal-containment structure is not offered as a primitive. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | There is no learning problem, loss or data model. The theorems are exact algebraic statements. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The statements contain no objective function, feasibility problem or approximation guarantee. The local-cohomology inequality is an extremality fact about a fixed ideal. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No communication, concurrency or fault model appears. The group actions used are algebraic symmetries of curves. |
| other | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The main value is mathematical: a Hilbert-function comparison and a degree bound for intersections with quadric complete intersections. Neither becomes an engineering construction without further work, and the hypotheses are restricted to characteristic zero. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-characteristic-zero** (hypothesis): The Hilbert-function comparison and the lex-plus-powers choice are proved only over coefficient fields of characteristic zero. Nothing in the paper transfers them to positive characteristic. Blocked use: ['Applying the monomial-ideal comparison over finite fields or in modular arithmetic', 'Quoting the local-cohomology inequality for a field of positive characteristic']
- **limit-degrees-at-least-two** (hypothesis): The commuting-form construction assumes every degree of the regular sequence is at least two. A sequence containing a linear form is outside the stated theorem. Blocked use: ['Feeding a sequence with a degree-one element to the construction', 'Claiming the ordered-basis conclusion for degrees below two']
- **limit-homogeneous-regular** (hypothesis): The ordered-basis criterion uses that the input is a homogeneous regular sequence. The Hilbert series product formula and the zero-dimensional quotient step both rely on this. Blocked use: ['Applying the criterion to inhomogeneous or affine polynomial systems', 'Applying it to sequences that are not regular']
- **limit-curve-exclusion** (hypothesis): The simple-factor property for the annihilator space is derived only when the characteristic set contains no integral curve of small degree. Without that exclusion the induction stage gives no multiplicity-one factor. Blocked use: ['Using the simple-factor lemma for relation ideals without the curve-exclusion check', 'Passing to the parameter-space theorem when the exclusion fails']
- **limit-block-count** (edge_case): The integrality and connectivity conclusions for the parameter variety hold only when the number of blocks is large enough relative to h and the dimension of W. Smaller block counts are not covered. Blocked use: ['Using the connectivity of the smooth cone with too few blocks', 'Choosing M before h and s are fixed']
- **limit-nonconstructive-existence** (nonconstructive): The construction is stated as an existence result through field extensions, descent and topological arguments. No finite procedure or complexity bound is given, so it cannot be treated as an algorithm. Blocked use: ['Quoting a running time for computing the forms or the monomial ideal', 'Implementing the construction as a library routine from the statements alone']
- **limit-complex-embeddings** (formal_scope): Complex embeddings of the centers are used only for topology and numerical comparison. Division is proved over the unembedded field, so conclusions that need the original constants fixed are not covered. Blocked use: ['Inferring division over a field from a complex specialization', 'Assuming embeddings chosen at different stages are compatible']
- **limit-cayley-bacharach-scope** (counterexample_scope): The Cayley–Bacharach degree bound is stated for zero-dimensional scheme-theoretic complete intersections of quadrics in characteristic zero. Other schemes are not covered. Blocked use: ['Applying the bound to non-complete intersections', 'Applying it to intersections of higher-degree hypersurfaces']

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We prove the Eisenbud–Green–Harris conjecture over every characteristic-zero field for homogeneous regular sequences of any positive length, with degrees at least two. Thus every homogeneous ideal containing such a sequence has the Hilbert function of a monomial ideal containing the corresponding pure powers.

<a id="pm-h-1"></a>
#### Statements index

<a id="sec:introduction"></a>
### Introduction

A homogeneous ideal is constrained both by the equations it contains and by the dimensions of its graded pieces. The Eisenbud–Green–Harris conjecture predicts a particularly concrete form for this constraint: a regular sequence can be replaced by the corresponding pure powers without changing the Hilbert function of a containing ideal. We prove this assertion over the complex numbers in its Artinian formulation, for arbitrary degrees, and then deduce the full characteristic-zero statement for regular sequences of arbitrary positive length. The main construction provides more than the Hilbert-function comparison: it replaces the polynomial variables by commuting linear forms with division-algebra coefficients and retains an ordered basis of the entire polynomial algebra.

Let $S=\mathbb C[x_1,\ldots,x_n]$ with its standard grading. For a homogeneous ideal $I\subseteq S$, write $$H_{S/I}(d)=\dim_\mathbb C(S/I)_d\qquad(d\ge0).$$ A sequence $f_1,\ldots,f_n$ of positive-degree homogeneous elements is *regular* if multiplication by $f_i$ is injective on $S/(f_1,\ldots,f_{i-1})$ for each $i$. Its quotient has Hilbert series $$\mathop{\mathrm{Hilb}}_{S/(f_1,\ldots,f_n)}(t)
 =\prod_{i=1}^n(1+t+\cdots+t^{a_i-1}),\qquad a_i=\deg f_i,$$ so every ideal containing it has an Artinian quotient.

Eisenbud, Green, and Harris arrived at the conjecture through extensions of Castelnuovo theory. Section 4 of <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[EGH93](#ref-EGH93)] formulates the quadratic case and then proposes the arbitrary-degree pure-power comparison. Their Cayley–Bacharach interpretation already appears in <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[EGH93](#ref-EGH93), Section 3] and is developed further in <a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[EGH96](#ref-EGH96), Section 2.1]. The pure-power model turns the geometric constraints into a problem about monomials with bounded exponents. The construction below supplies that model for an arbitrary homogeneous regular sequence.

We first state this construction. If $k/\mathbb C$ is a field extension and $\Delta$ is a central division $k$-algebra, then $\Delta[x_1,\ldots,x_n]$ denotes the polynomial algebra with central variables $x_j$. The coefficients in $\Delta$ have degree zero. A collection of its linear forms may commute even when their individual coefficients do not.

<a id="stmt-thm-construction"></a>
<div id="thm:construction" class="theorem">

**Theorem 1** (Commuting-form construction). *Let $n\ge1$, let $2\le a_1\le\cdots\le a_n$, and let $f_1,\ldots,f_n$ be a homogeneous regular sequence in $\mathbb C[x_1,\ldots,x_n]$ with $\deg f_i=a_i$. There exist a finitely generated field extension $k/\mathbb C$, a finite-dimensional central division $k$-algebra $\Delta$, and a commutative graded $k$-subalgebra $$k[x_1,\ldots,x_n]\subseteq A\subseteq\Delta[x_1,\ldots,x_n]$$ generated by finitely many pairwise commuting linear forms, with distinguished $t_1,\ldots,t_n\in A_1$, such that <a id="eq:construction-triangular"></a>$$\label{eq:construction-triangular}
 t_i^{a_i}=c_i f_i+\sum_{l<i}B_{il}f_l+\sum_{d>i}C_{id}t_d
 \qquad(1\le i\le n).$$ Here $c_i\in k^\times$, $B_{il}\in A_{a_i-a_l}$, and $C_{id}\in A_{a_i-1}$. Moreover, $A$ is finite over $k[x]$, and $$\{t_1^{\alpha_1}\cdots t_n^{\alpha_n}:
             \alpha\in\mathbb Z_{\ge0}^n\}$$ is a basis of the full polynomial algebra $\Delta[x]$ as a left $\Delta$-vector space.*

</div>

The basis is not restricted to the complete-intersection quotient. In particular, it describes every graded piece before any additional ideal is imposed. This is the interface used for resolution transfer in the companion paper <a id="pm-cite-4"></a>[[BettiCompanion](#ref-BettiCompanion), Lemma 4.1]. For Hilbert functions, a least-exponent argument already gives the following consequence.

<a id="stmt-thm-main"></a>
<div id="thm:main" class="corollary">

**Corollary 2** (Artinian Eisenbud–Green–Harris). *Let $n\ge1$ and $2\le a_1\le\cdots\le a_n$. Suppose that a homogeneous ideal $I\subseteq\mathbb C[x_1,\ldots,x_n]$ contains a regular sequence $f_1,\ldots,f_n$ with $\deg f_i=a_i$. There is a monomial ideal $J\subseteq\mathbb C[x_1,\ldots,x_n]$ such that $$x_i^{a_i}\in J\quad(1\le i\le n),
 \qquad
 H_{S/J}(d)=H_{S/I}(d)\quad\text{for every }d\ge0.$$*

</div>

Thus the Artinian Eisenbud–Green–Harris conjecture is resolved positively over $\mathbb C$. One ideal $J$ realizes the entire Hilbert function, and there is no restriction on the further generators of $I$.

To describe the lex-plus-powers formulation, order variables by $x_1>\cdots>x_n$, and let $J$ be the monomial ideal in the corollary. In degree $d$, among monomials outside $P=(x_1^{a_1},\ldots,x_n^{a_n})$, take the $q_d=\dim_\mathbb CJ_d-\dim_\mathbb CP_d$ lexicographically largest ones. The Clements–Lindström theorem says that these degreewise choices, together with $P$, form an ideal whenever the Hilbert function comes from an ideal containing $P$ <a id="pm-cite-5"></a>[[CL69](#ref-CL69)]. Thus Corollary <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">2</a> also gives one lex-plus-powers ideal with the entire prescribed Hilbert function. We use the exact compression statement in <a id="pm-cite-6"></a>[[CDS22](#ref-CDS22), Theorem 2.1(i)]; it is not a premise of Theorem <a href="#thm:construction" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:construction">1</a>.

The Hilbert-function conclusion is not restricted to Artinian quotients or to the coefficient field $\mathbb C$.

<a id="stmt-cor-characteristic-zero"></a>
<div id="cor:characteristic-zero" class="corollary">

**Corollary 3** (Eisenbud–Green–Harris in characteristic zero). *Let $F$ be a field of characteristic zero, let $S_F=F[x_1,\ldots,x_n]$ be standard graded, and let $$1\le c\le n,\qquad 2\le a_1\le\cdots\le a_c.$$ If a homogeneous ideal $I\subseteq S_F$ contains a homogeneous regular sequence $f_1,\ldots,f_c$ with $\deg f_i=a_i$, there is a monomial ideal $J\subseteq S_F$ containing $P_F=(x_1^{a_1},\ldots,x_c^{a_c})$ such that $$\dim_F(S_F/J)_d=\dim_F(S_F/I)_d\qquad(d\ge0).$$ Moreover, $J$ may be chosen lex-plus-powers: in each degree its monomials outside $P_F$ are the lexicographically greatest $\dim_F I_d-\dim_F(P_F)_d$ monomials outside $P_F$, for the order $x_1>\cdots>x_n$.*

</div>

The proof in Section <a href="#subsec:generality" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="subsec:generality">9.1</a> combines the Artinian result with a reduction to full regular sequences and descent through a finitely generated coefficient field. The division-algebra construction of Theorem <a href="#thm:construction" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:construction">1</a> itself concerns full regular sequences over $\mathbb C$. Section <a href="#sec:consequences" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:consequences">9</a> also gives local-cohomology inequalities for the resulting lex-plus-powers ideal and a scheme-theoretic quadratic Cayley–Bacharach bound.

<a id="pm-h-3"></a>
#### Earlier results on EGH

Results depending only on the degrees of the regular sequence have covered important ranges. Caviglia and Maclagan proved EGH when $a_i>\sum_{j<i}(a_j-1)$ for $i\ge3$ <a id="pm-cite-7"></a>[[CM08](#ref-CM08)]; Caviglia and De Stefani extended this to the non-strict inequalities <a id="pm-cite-8"></a>[[CDeS20](#ref-CDeS20), Theorem A]. For quadratic sequences, Chen proved the case of at most four variables <a id="pm-cite-9"></a>[[Chen12](#ref-Chen12), Theorem 2.2]. Caviglia, De Stefani, and Sbarra give an independent proof for five quadrics, alongside a systematic account of the conjecture <a id="pm-cite-10"></a>[[CDS22](#ref-CDS22), Theorem 4.14].

Other results use additional structure of the forms or the containing ideal. Abedelfatah proves EGH when all forms of the regular sequence split into linear factors <a id="pm-cite-11"></a>[[Abedelfatah15](#ref-Abedelfatah15), Corollary 4.3]. Cooper treats subsets of finite reduced complete intersections of types $(a,b)$, $(2,a,b)$, and $(3,a,a)$ <a id="pm-cite-12"></a>[[Cooper12](#ref-Cooper12)]. Chong uses liaison to treat sequentially bounded licci ideals with a minimal first link and a minimally contained regular sequence; his results include height-three Gorenstein ideals under the corresponding minimal-containment condition <a id="pm-cite-13"></a>[[Chong16](#ref-Chong16), Theorem 3 and Corollary 11].

Recent work addresses both small-degree cases and asymptotic ranges. Abedelfatah proves EGH for quadratic almost complete intersections in six variables <a id="pm-cite-14"></a>[[Abedelfatah26](#ref-Abedelfatah26), Theorem 4.3]. Kuzmanovski obtains Hilbert-function comparisons through prescribed degree ranges when the initial degree is sufficiently large <a id="pm-cite-15"></a>[[Kuzmanovski26](#ref-Kuzmanovski26), Theorems 1.4–1.5]. The construction here applies to arbitrary regular sequences of the stated degrees and gives one ideal with the same Hilbert function in every degree.

<a id="pm-h-4"></a>
#### Proof strategy and its methods

The triangular identities in Theorem <a href="#thm:construction" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:construction">1</a> make the $t_i$ a regular system of parameters on the finite module $\Delta[x]$ over $A$. Since they have degree one, a Hilbert-series count gives the full ordered basis. Least exponents of elements of an extended ideal then form one upward-closed set, defining the ordinary monomial ideal. Section <a href="#sec:reduction" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:reduction">2</a> establishes this algebraic reduction before the construction begins.

We construct the rows from $i=n$ down to $i=1$. At a stage with $b=a_i$, the aim is to express a suitable right-hand side of the new row as a sum $\ell_1^b+\cdots+\ell_M^b$ of powers of existing forms, then combine these powers two at a time until the sum becomes $t_i^b$. Two difficulties govern the construction: the binary addition matrices must have coefficients in a division algebra containing the old coefficients, and the earlier power identities must remain available when the next degree is smaller.

To retain those identities, we use a finite-dimensional space $U$ of formal linear forms containing the variables $x_j$ and the forms $t_d$ already constructed, together with an ideal $Q\subseteq\mathop{\mathrm{Sym}}U$ generated in degrees one and two. Evaluation recovers the actual commuting forms, and every earlier identity holds modulo $Q$. Let $\mathfrak j_b$ be the degree-$b$ part of $$(Q,f_1,\ldots,f_i,t_{i+1},\ldots,t_n)\subseteq\mathop{\mathrm{Sym}}U.$$ A linear functional $\lambda$ on $\mathop{\mathrm{Sym}}^b U$ that annihilates $\mathfrak j_b$ defines the degree-$b$ polynomial $w_\lambda(\ell)=\lambda(\ell^b)$ on $U$. If $W$ is their space, then $$\sum_{m=1}^M\ell_m^b\in\mathfrak j_b
 \quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
 \sum_{m=1}^M w(\ell_m)=0\qquad(w\in W).$$ Let $X\subseteq\mathbb P(U^{\oplus M})$ be the projectivization of the common zero set of these equations.

The geometric safeguard is that the projective zero set of $Q$ contains no curve of bounded small degree. Section <a href="#sec:low-relations" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:low-relations">3</a> constructs such data from independent quadratic equations, proves that finite homogeneous enlargement preserves the property, and uses it to show that every nonzero $w\in W$ has an irreducible factor of multiplicity one. For sufficiently many blocks, Section <a href="#sec:parameters" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:parameters">4</a> then proves that $X$ is an integral complete intersection and that the nonzero cone over its smooth locus has arbitrarily high connectivity. The argument allows nonisolated singularities and requires only one simple factor in each member of $W$.

The binary addition matrices come from a curve with three sections satisfying $z^b=u^b+v^b$. A generic degree-zero line bundle makes multiplication by these sections linear in $u,v$. This use of matrix pencils belongs to the generalized Clifford-algebra and vector-bundle correspondence developed by van den Bergh <a id="pm-cite-16"></a>[[VanDenBergh87](#ref-VanDenBergh87)] and Kulkarni <a id="pm-cite-17"></a>[[Kulkarni03](#ref-Kulkarni03)]; the trivial finite-pushforward formulation appears in <a id="pm-cite-18"></a>[[CKM12](#ref-CKM12), Proposition 2.5]. Section <a href="#sec:curves" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:curves">5</a> constructs the required curve and pencils explicitly, together with a finite group action. A matrix representation alone does not make the coefficient algebra division.

The two Picard varieties of the curve serve different purposes: degree-zero line bundles parametrize the matrices, while the degree-one Picard variety supplies a free group action and maps carrying prescribed characters. Equivariant descent first gives a central simple algebra. To prove that it is division, we show that the dimension of every finite right module is divisible by the dimension of the algebra, both measured over its center. A nonzero proper right ideal would contradict this divisibility.

Section <a href="#sec:descent" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:descent">6</a> uses a determinant-of-cohomology correction to relate module dimensions to Euler characteristics on a product of Picard varieties. The free action also gives divisibility of Euler characteristics for the corresponding family over $X$, after twisting by $\mathcal O_X(l)$. The correction uses the formalism of Knudsen and Mumford <a id="pm-cite-19"></a>[[KnudsenMumford76](#ref-KnudsenMumford76)], and its numerical effect follows from Grothendieck’s Riemann–Roch theorem <a id="pm-cite-20"></a>[[BorelSerre58](#ref-BorelSerre58)]. Section <a href="#sec:index" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:index">7</a> uses the character maps, connectivity of the smooth cone, and integral topological $K$-theory to compare these Euler characteristics and prove that the algebra is division while retaining the old coefficient algebra.

The use of highly connected algebraic parameter spaces and finite quotients is related to the Godeaux–Serre approximation method <a id="pm-cite-21"></a>[[Totaro99](#ref-Totaro99), Section 5]. Topological restrictions on an algebraic index have a close precedent in Antieau and Williams <a id="pm-cite-22"></a>[[AntieauWilliams13](#ref-AntieauWilliams13), Theorem 3.4]. Here the determinant correction and integral $K$-theory pushforward separate divisibility by the new group order from divisibility by the old algebra’s degree. Consequently the required connectivity can be fixed before enlarging the number of summands. Finally, Section <a href="#sec:assembly" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:assembly">8</a> verifies the characters that make the new output invariant and retains the new row through a finite extension defined by linear and quadratic relations. This closes the induction.

When every $a_i=2$, Corollary <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">2</a> gives the quadratic assertion. The complete-intersection Hilbert series is then $(1+t)^n$, and the standard monomials of a monomial ideal containing $x_1^2,\ldots,x_n^2$ correspond to a family of subsets of $\{1,\ldots,n\}$ closed under taking subsets. Here this special case follows from the same ordered-basis construction as all other degree sequences; no separate quadratic theorem is required.

Figure <a href="#fig:construction" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="fig:construction">1</a> records the dependencies within one induction step and the return to its hypotheses before the next row.

<figure id="fig:construction">

<figcaption>The induction separates the geometry of the constraints from the binary addition matrices. The return arrow preserves the hypotheses for the next row; monomial extraction is performed only after the construction is complete.</figcaption>
</figure>
